The tensors are stacked across cells in the batch: <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-43"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-43.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-43.gif"/></noscript></span></span> and <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-44"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-44.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-44.gif"/></noscript></span></span>, with resulting shape (<em>B ×</em> 1 <em>×</em> 1280). The gene-level loss is:
<span class="disp-formula" id="disp-formula-35"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-39.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-39.gif"/></noscript></span>

</span>
and therefore it effectively measures the similarity between predicted and true gene expression patterns within each cell <em>b</em> (which is then averaged across cells in ℒ<sub>gene</sub>).</p><p id="p-100">To capture variation in gene expression across cells in the mini-batch, we also compute a cell-level loss using the shared subset of genes ℛ. Let <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-45"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-45.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-45.gif"/></noscript></span></span> and <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-46"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-46.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-46.gif"/></noscript></span></span> denote the predicted and true expression values in cell <em>i</em> for the shared genes ℛ. These tensors are stacked across cells in the batch and transposed: <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-47"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-47.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-47.gif"/></noscript></span></span> and <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-48"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-48.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-48.gif"/></noscript></span></span>, resulting in shape (|ℛ| <em>×</em> 1 <em>× B</em>), with |ℛ| = 256. We then compute the distance between the concatenated predictions and targets across all cells in the batch for each gene. Specifically, we consider the r-th gene (row) of the transposed tensors, denoted as <strong>Ŝ</strong><sup>′(<em>r</em>)</sup> and <strong>S</strong><sup>′(<em>r</em>)</sup> (each being a 1 × <em>B</em> vector):
<span class="disp-formula" id="disp-formula-36"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-40.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-40.gif"/></noscript></span>

</span>
and therefore it effectively measures the similarity between predicted and true gene expression across cells in the batch for each gene in ℛ (which is then averaged across genes in ℒ<sub>cell</sub>).</p><p id="p-101">The final training loss for expression prediction combines both axes:
<span class="disp-formula" id="disp-formula-37"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-41.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-41.gif"/></noscript></span>

</span>
This dual-axis reconstruction loss captures both gene-wise reconstruction fidelity within each cell and the consistency of expression patterns across cells for shared genes.</p></div><div id="sec-32" class="subsection"><h5>Dataset Classification Modeling</h5><p id="p-102">To disentangle dataset-specific technical effects from biological variation, we introduce an auxiliary dataset prediction task. Using the <kbd>[DS]</kbd> token embedding, the model predicts the dataset of origin:
<span class="disp-formula" id="disp-formula-38"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-42.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-42.gif"/></noscript></span>

</span>
with <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-49"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-49.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-49.gif"/></noscript></span></span> as in <strong><a id="xref-disp-formula-32-1" class="xref-disp-formula" href="#disp-formula-32">Eq. 30</a></strong>. We employ cross-entropy loss:
<span class="disp-formula" id="disp-formula-39"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-43.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-43.gif"/></noscript></span>

</span>
where <em>d</em><sup>(<em>b</em>)</sup> denotes the true dataset label for cell <em>b</em> in the batch, and <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-50"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-50.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-50.gif"/></noscript></span></span> is the predicted label.</p><p id="p-103">In our implementation, cells within the same AnnData file are treated as originating from the same dataset for this classification task. For our SE model trained on Arc scBaseCount, CZ CELLxGENE, and Tahoe-100M, this task becomes a multi-class classification problem over approximately 14,000 datasets. This auxiliary objective encourages the model to pool the relevant information in this token position, disentangling it from true biological signal. Empirically, since datasets in scBaseCount are organized by Sequence Read Archive experiment identifiers (SRX), which typically correspond to individual cell types or experimental conditions, the dataset token captures cell type and cell line information alongside technical batch effects. Since this token embedding is derived exclusively from the expression set representation without incorporating metadata, the trained model can be used at inference time without requiring explicit specification of technical parameters.</p></div><div id="sec-33" class="subsection"><h5>Total Loss</h5><p id="p-104">The SE model is trained using a combination of both losses:
<span class="disp-formula" id="disp-formula-40"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-44.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/graphic-44.gif"/></noscript></span>

</span>
</p></div></div></div><div id="sec-34" class="subsection"><h3>4.5. Datasets</h3><div id="sec-35" class="subsection"><h4>4.5.1. Datasets Used for ST Training</h4><p id="p-105">We used several single-cell perturbation datasets in this study: Tahoe-100M dataset (<a id="xref-ref-120-10" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-120">Zhang et al., 2025</a>), the Replogle-Nadig dataset (<a id="xref-ref-90-7" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-90">Replogle et al., 2022</a>; <a id="xref-ref-75-4" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-75">Nadig et al., 2025</a>), the Parse-PBMC dataset (<a id="xref-ref-81-5" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-81">Parse Biosciences, 2023</a>), the Jiang dataset (<a id="xref-ref-51-1" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-51">Jiang et al., 2020</a>), the McFaline dataset (<a id="xref-ref-73-1" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-73">McFaline-Figueroa et al., 2019</a>), and the Srivatsan dataset (<a id="xref-ref-102-4" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-102">Srivatsan et al., 2020</a>) (<a id="xref-table-wrap-1-1" class="xref-table" href="#T1">Table 1</a>). All datasets were filtered to retain measurements for 19,790 human protein-coding Ensembl genes and subsequently normalized to a total UMI depth of 10,000. Raw count data were log-transformed using <kbd>scanpy.pp.log1p</kbd>. For analyses on highly variable genes (HVGs) throughout this work, for each dataset, the top 2,000 HVGs were identified using <kbd>scanpy.pp.highly_variable_genes</kbd>. Log-transformed expression values for these HVGs were used as gene-level features. PCA embeddings of cells were computed using <kbd>scanpy.pp.pca</kbd>.</p><div id="T1" class="table pos-float"><div class="table-inline table-callout-links"><div class="callout"><span>View this table:</span><ul class="callout-links"><li class="view-inline first"><a href="" class="table-expand-inline" data-table-url="/highwire/markup/4740560/expansion?postprocessors=highwire_tables%2Chighwire_reclass%2Chighwire_figures%2Chighwire_math%2Chighwire_inline_linked_media%2Chighwire_embed&amp;table-expand-inline=1" data-icon-position="" data-hide-link-title="0">View inline</a></li><li class="view-popup"><a href="/highwire/markup/4740560/expansion?width=1000&amp;height=500&amp;iframe=true&amp;postprocessors=highwire_tables%2Chighwire_reclass%2Chighwire_figures%2Chighwire_math%2Chighwire_inline_linked_media%2Chighwire_embed" class="colorbox colorbox-load table-expand-popup" rel="gallery-fragment-tables" data-icon-position="" data-hide-link-title="0">View popup</a></li><li class="download-ppt last"><a href="/highwire/powerpoint/4740560" class="highwire-figure-link highwire-figure-link-ppt" data-icon-position="" data-hide-link-title="0">Download powerpoint</a></li></ul></div></div><div class="table-caption"><span class="table-label">Table 1:</span> <span class="caption-title">Summary of datasets used in ST experiments, including total number of cells, perturbations, and biological contexts (e.g., donors, cell types, or conditions).</span><div class="sb-div caption-clear"></div></div></div></div><div id="sec-36" class="subsection"><h4>4.5.2. Additional preprocessing for genetic perturbation datasets</h4><p id="p-106">Genetic perturbation datasets were further filtered to only retain perturbations with high knockdown efficacy using the <kbd>filter_on_target_knockdown</kbd> function from the Cell-Load package. The following three filtering steps were performed:</p><ul class="list-unord " id="list-4"><li id="list-item-9"><p id="p-107"><strong>Perturbation-level filtering</strong>: Retain only those perturbations (except controls) whose average knockdown efficiency meets a minimum threshold, residual expression is <em>≤</em> 0.30.</p></li><li id="list-item-10"><p id="p-108"><strong>Cell-level filtering</strong>: Within the selected perturbations, keep only those cells that individually meet a stricter knockdown threshold, residual expression <em>≤</em> 0.50.</p></li><li id="list-item-11"><p id="p-109"><strong>Minimum cell count</strong>: Drop any perturbations that have fewer than a specified number of remaining valid cells (30), while always preserving control cells.</p></li></ul></div><div id="sec-37" class="subsection"><h4>4.5.3. Datasets Used for SE Training</h4><p id="p-110">SE was trained on 167 million human cells across the Arc scBaseCount (<a id="xref-ref-115-5" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-115">Youngblut et al., 2025</a>), CZ CELL×GENE (<a id="xref-ref-87-4" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-87">Program et al., 2025</a>), Tahoe-100M (<a id="xref-ref-120-11" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-120">Zhang et al., 2025</a>) datasets (<a id="xref-table-wrap-2-1" class="xref-table" href="#T2">Table 2</a>). To avoid data leakage in our context generalization benchmarks, we trained on 20 Tahoe-100M cell lines separate from the five held out cell lines from (<strong><a id="xref-fig-2-16" class="xref-fig" href="#F2">Fig. 2</a></strong>). scBaseCount data was filtered to only retain cells with at least 1,000 non-zero expression measurements and 2,000 UMIs per cell. A subset of AnnData files were left out for computing validation loss.</p><div id="T2" class="table pos-float"><div class="table-inline table-callout-links"><div class="callout"><span>View this table:</span><ul class="callout-links"><li class="view-inline first"><a href="" class="table-expand-inline" data-table-url="/highwire/markup/4740562/expansion?postprocessors=highwire_tables%2Chighwire_reclass%2Chighwire_figures%2Chighwire_math%2Chighwire_inline_linked_media%2Chighwire_embed&amp;table-expand-inline=1" data-icon-position="" data-hide-link-title="0">View inline</a></li><li class="view-popup"><a href="/highwire/markup/4740562/expansion?width=1000&amp;height=500&amp;iframe=true&amp;postprocessors=highwire_tables%2Chighwire_reclass%2Chighwire_figures%2Chighwire_math%2Chighwire_inline_linked_media%2Chighwire_embed" class="colorbox colorbox-load table-expand-popup" rel="gallery-fragment-tables" data-icon-position="" data-hide-link-title="0">View popup</a></li><li class="download-ppt last"><a href="/highwire/powerpoint/4740562" class="highwire-figure-link highwire-figure-link-ppt" data-icon-position="" data-hide-link-title="0">Download powerpoint</a></li></ul></div></div><div class="table-caption"><span class="table-label">Table 2:</span> <span class="caption-title">Summary of datasets used in SE experiments, showing total number of cells.</span><div class="sb-div caption-clear"></div></div></div></div></div><div id="sec-38" class="subsection"><h3>4.6. Training</h3><p id="p-111">All models are implemented using PyTorch Lightning with distributed data parallel (DDP) training. We use PyTorch’s automatic mixed precision (AMP) to reduce memory usage and accelerate training. During inference, genes not present in the embedding vocabulary are ignored.</p><div id="sec-39" class="subsection"><h4>4.6.1. ST Hyperparameters</h4><p id="p-112">The ST architecture utilizes a shared hidden dimension, denoted as <em>h</em>, across its modules. Each encoders maps its respective inputs to this <em>h</em>-dimensional space, which also serves as the internal dimension for the transformer layers. The core transformer module, <em>f</em><sub>ST</sub>, is either based on a LLaMA (<a id="xref-ref-106-1" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-106">Touvron et al., 2023</a>) backbone or a GPT2 (<a id="xref-ref-89-1" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-89">Radford et al., 2019</a>) backbone, provided in HuggingFace. We use a GPT2 backbone for sparser datasets, such as Replogle-Nadig (<a id="xref-ref-90-8" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-90">Replogle et al., 2022</a>; <a id="xref-ref-75-5" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-75">Nadig et al., 2025</a>), due to LayerNorm’s desirable centering transform operations that revive dead neurons in low-data regimes (<a id="xref-ref-71-1" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-71">Lyle et al., 2024</a>). All models are modified to use bi-directional attention. Dimensionality and parameterization of the backbone used in <strong><a id="xref-fig-2-17" class="xref-fig" href="#F2">Fig. 2</a></strong> are provided in <a id="xref-table-wrap-3-1" class="xref-table" href="#T3">Table 3</a>. Since cell order within each set is arbitrary, no positional encodings are used. Additionally, dropout is not applied within the transformer. Additionally, as we work directly in vector space, we deactivate the model parameters allocated for word embeddings. Before training, most ST weights are initialized sampling from Kaiming Uniform <span class="inline-formula" id="inline-formula-51"><span class="highwire-responsive-lazyload"><img src="data:image/gif;base64,R0lGODlhAQABAIAAAAAAAP///yH5BAEAAAAALAAAAAABAAEAAAIBRAA7" class="highwire-embed lazyload" alt="Embedded Image" data-src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-51.gif"/><noscript><img class="highwire-embed" alt="Embedded Image" src="https://www.biorxiv.org/sites/default/files/highwire/biorxiv/early/2025/07/10/2025.06.26.661135/embed/inline-graphic-51.gif"/></noscript></span></span> (<a id="xref-ref-44-1" class="xref-bibr" href="#ref-44">He et al., 2015</a>) with the exception of the transformer backbone which is initialized from 𝒩 (0, 0.02<sup>2</sup>). Detailed configurations for each neural network module are summarized in <a id="xref-table-wrap-4-1" class="xref-table" href="#T4">Table 4</a>.</p><div id="T3" class="table pos-float"><div class="table-inline table-callout-links"><div class="callout"><span>View this table:</span><ul class="callout-links"><li class="view-inline first"><a href="" class="table-expand-inline"
